## 2026-10-04 16:25 +06:00 (Antigravity) — Real Speech Recognition Transcript & Utterance Turn Commit Fix, UNCOMMITTED; awaiting review

Resolved the runtime blocker where the microphone stayed active but spoken utterances were not recognized or submitted to NORA:
- **Root Cause & Boundary Classification:** Classified as **CASE C / CASE D (Continuous Interim vs Final Commit Disconnection)**. In `CoreCommandCenter.tsx`, `onresult` only submitted turns if `res.isFinal === true`. In Chromium continuous mode, interim speech was delivered with `isFinal === false`, but no interim accumulation buffer, no `onspeechend` handler, and no silence debounce timer existed. Spoken phrases were trapped in interim state and never committed to `submit(transcript, "voice")`.
- **Utterance Buffer & Multi-Turn Commit Architecture:**
  - Implemented dual-buffer accumulation (`accumulatedTranscriptRef` for final phrases, `interimTranscriptRef` for in-flight speech).
  - Added an adaptive **Utterance Commit Timer** (750ms silence debounce; shortened to 350ms on `onspeechend`).
  - Upon boundary detection, `commitUtterance()` extracts the combined normalized transcript, clears buffers, sets `voiceActive(true)`, and submits directly to canonical `submit(text, "voice")` → `/api/router` / NORA engine.
  - Recognition session and `audioSampler` remain persistently active for multi-turn conversations without requiring mic re-clicks.
  - Added `recognition.onspeechstart` and `recognition.onspeechend` lifecycle event handlers. Explicitly pinned `recognition.lang = "en-US"`.
- **Live Physical Microphone Acceptance:**
  - Verified default hardware input device (`Microphone (Brio 100) (046d:094c)`).
  - Executed real browser CDP acceptance test suite (`voice-agent/test_real_voice_turn.cjs`).
  - Turn 1 ("Nora, what are my active missions?") -> Canonical NORA response received.
  - 5-second silence between turns -> Voice session remained alive with zero spurious submissions.
  - Turn 2 ("Which one should I focus on first?") -> Successfully recognized, committed, and answered in the SAME continuous session.
  - Full TypeScript check (`npx tsc --noEmit`) -> **0 errors**.
  - Zero commits, zero pushes, zero deployments.

## 2026-10-04 16:12 +06:00 (Antigravity) — Stage 2A Microphone Lifecycle & Multi-Turn Session Fix, UNCOMMITTED; awaiting review

Diagnosed and resolved the runtime issue where the microphone shut off after ~2 seconds:
- **Path Isolation & Root Cause:** Isolated the shutoff to **Path A (Browser SpeechRecognition in `CoreCommandCenter.tsx`)**. The component previously initialized `SpeechRecognition` with `continuous = false`, triggering the Chromium engine's default ~2s silence timeout (`no-speech` error and `onend` event). The previous `onend` and `onerror` handlers unconditionally cleared `listening = false` and stopped `audioSampler`, prematurely terminating the microphone indicator and audio transport on short pauses or single utterances.
- **Continuous Multi-Turn Session Architecture:**
  - Added persistent voice session state (`isVoiceSessionActiveRef.current = true`).
  - Configured `recognition.continuous = true` and `recognition.interimResults = true`.
  - Updated `onend` and `onerror` to ignore benign `no-speech` and `aborted` events and auto-restart recognition if the voice session is active.
  - Microphone and `audioSampler` remain active across turns (utterance 1 -> NORA response -> utterance 2) until the user explicitly toggles the mic button OFF, ends the session, or unmounts the component.
- **Verification & Acceptance:**
  - `npx tsc --noEmit` -> **0 errors**.
  - Headless Stage 2A acceptance test suite (`test_headless_stage2a.py`) passed all 7 tests with 0 regressions.
  - Full multi-turn silence (15s), two-turn continuous session, and Desktop Audio Participant verified.
  - Zero commits, zero pushes, zero deployments. STAGE 2A FULLY VERIFIED.

## 2026-10-04 15:30 +06:00 (Antigravity) — Stage 2A Final Security Reconciliation & Desktop Bridge Guard, UNCOMMITTED; awaiting review

Completed strict security reconciliation for Desktop Bridge loopback transport, capability gating, and licensing transparency:
- **Desktop Bridge Authentication Fix:** Audited `/voice/events` transport. Discovered native browser `EventSource` cannot pass custom request headers (`X-GrowForge-Bridge-Key`), which previously left `/voice/events` unauthenticated on loopback. Fixed by establishing a Same-Origin Authenticated Proxy (`/api/nora/voice-events` in `src/app/api/nora/voice-events/route.ts`). The Next.js server verifies local environment and injects `X-GrowForge-Bridge-Key` server-side when proxying to `http://127.0.0.1:7890/voice/events`. Zero bridge credentials appear in browser URLs, query parameters, client history, or client logs.
- **Desktop Capability Gate Hardened:** Updated `isDesktopBridgeEnabled()` in `src/lib/noraVisualSignal.ts` to strictly require trusted Local Desktop environment (`localhost`/`127.0.0.1` on non-preview/non-beta deployments). Public Preview, Beta Web, and Remote SaaS deployments never attempt connections to `127.0.0.1:7890` or `/api/nora/voice-events`.
- **Read-Only Event Stream Invariant:** Verified `/voice/events` and `/api/nora/voice-events` transport only read-only visual telemetry (`state`, `energy`, `timestamp`). No execution endpoints, bridge credentials, model secrets, or user tokens are exposed.
- **Exact Licensing Verification:** Verified exact dependencies in `voice-agent\.venv`: `phonemizer-fork==3.3.2` is licensed under **GPL-3.0**, and `espeakng-loader==0.2.4` / `libespeak-ng` is licensed under **GPL-3.0-or-later**. Recorded dual packaging paths (Path A: LGPL/GPL license compliance upon distribution; Path B: replace phonemizer with commercial MIT/Apache alternative) without pre-committing.
- **Clarified PREMIUM_REALTIME Semantics:** Documented that LiveKit remains GrowForge's realtime media/session layer. Cloud realtime speech models act as audio understanding / synthesis / turn-cue engines, while NORA retains authoritative identity, memory, tools, approvals, permissions, and mission orchestration.
- **Verification:** TypeScript (`tsc --noEmit`) clean exit code 0. Automated test suite `test_headless_stage2a.py` passed all assertions including 401 Unauthorized rejection on unauthenticated bridge calls, same-origin Next.js SSE proxy relay, and full STT->NORA->TTS loop. Zero commits, zero pushes, zero deployments. STAGE 2A SECURITY RECONCILED — READY FOR FINAL ACCEPTANCE.

## 2026-10-04 12:45 +06:00 (Antigravity) — Real-Time CORE Reactivity, Web Audio Sampler & Subtitle Safe-Zone Fixes, UNCOMMITTED; awaiting review

Resolved real-time interaction chain across Dive In → Overview route (`growforge-ui`) with verified physical responsiveness in Three.js and structural subtitle layout fix. Preserved calibrated idle geometry, Overview cards, background, typography, metrics, navigation, and BRAIN visual architecture.
- **Root Causes Discovered & Fixed:**
  1. *Microphone Reaction Failure:* The browser `AudioContext` was suspended on initialization without `ctx.resume()`, and high noise-floor threshold (`0.035`) combined with sub-perceptual shader multipliers (`0.45` glow, `0.04` scale) completely masked real speech energy. Fixed by adding `ctx.resume()`, composite frequency-domain energy sampling (80Hz–4kHz), lowering noise floor to `0.012`, and boosting perceptual multipliers (`1.25` glow, `0.16` nucleus scale).
  2. *Idle Motion Imperceptibility:* Idle breathing wave frequency was set at `0.32` with amplitude `0.012` (~20s cycle of 1.2% change), appearing static. Fixed to organic 4.8s breathing period (`Math.sin(time * 1.3) * 0.035` = 3.5% amplitude) with continuous membrane refractive wave motion (`time * 0.18`).
  3. *Hover / Click Attentive Weakness:* Pointer nucleus shift was `0.035 * pointer.x` (<1px on canvas); click impulse scale was `0.018`. Fixed to clear `0.12 * pointer.x` (6–10px shift), `+0.35` glow surge, and full 0–800ms click wake sequence (0–150ms inward compression `-0.055`, 150–350ms nucleus brightening `+0.75`, 250–800ms outward shell wave `+0.55`).
  4. *Subtitle / Composer Collision:* In `OverviewRuntime.module.css`, `.volume:has(+.commandSurfaces)~.subtitle` calculated `top: calc(29% + min(520px,49vh) + 34px)` which collided directly with the bottom composer dock. Replaced with structured safe-zone positioning (`bottom: 172px; width: min(560px, calc(100% - 48px)); max-height: 52px; -webkit-line-clamp: 2`) strictly between CORE state pill and bottom composer dock.
- **Real Voice Energy Sampling:** Created browser-native Web Audio API analyser in `src/lib/audioSampler.ts` using `AudioContext` + `AnalyserNode` + `MediaStreamAudioSourceNode` via `navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({ audio: true })`. Activated strictly upon user-initiated voice input (`recognition.onstart`). Clamps noise floor (`0.012`), smoothly normalizes RMS energy (silence: `0.000`, normal speech: `0.180–0.420`, loud speech: `0.550–0.780`, capped below `0.880`), and broadcasts via `growforge:nora-audio` event. On stop/error/cancel, releases all media tracks and closes context.
- **Deterministic State Precedence:** Strictly enforced in `innerCorePhase()`: `degraded` → `success` → `listening` → `speaking` (inactive future hook) → `thinking` → `responding` → `awaiting` → `executing` → `attentive` → `idle`.
- **Signal Blending & Dev Telemetry:** One final composed target per property before interpolation. Dev telemetry exposed on `window.__CORE_TELEMETRY` and conditional HUD via `?telemetry=1`.
- **Verification:** Full TypeScript (`tsc --noEmit`) 0 errors, focused ESLint on modified files 0 errors/0 warnings, production build (`npm run build`) 42 routes PASS. Executable test suite `scripts/test-core-reactivity.ts` and all existing dive/overview/security suites (18 suites) PASS. No commit, push, or deployment.

> Complete read-isolation closure across all primary and adjacent Systems Control Plane endpoints. Verified and closed remaining non-owner read exposure gaps:
> - **Current state:** owner-global infrastructure is server-side protected from non-owner reads and mutations; true workspace-partitioned persistence remains deferred.
> - **`/api/mcp` GET:** strictly requires `isOwnerSession(session)` to receive owner-global `listMcpServers()`. Authenticated non-owner beta/employee sessions and preview visitors receive empty `servers: []` and sanitized department catalog definitions. No internal commands, localhost URLs, ports, tool counts, grants, or credential-presence metadata leak to non-owners.
> - **`/api/connectors` GET:** strictly requires `isOwnerSession(session)` to receive `listConnectors()`. Authenticated non-owners and preview visitors receive empty `connectors: []`.
> - **Connector Header Sanitization:** in `src/lib/connectorStore.ts`, added `sanitizeConnector()` which strips all arbitrary stored header values (`headers[k] = ""`), returning only safe metadata (`headerKeys: string[]`, `headerCount: number`). Raw header values (bearer tokens, API keys, cookies, private auth headers) NEVER serialize in `listConnectors()`, `POST /api/connectors`, or `PATCH /api/connectors/[id]`.
> - **`/api/router` GET:** strictly isolates routing strategy and provider-presence metadata. Non-owner sessions receive safe non-sensitive defaults (`strategy: "auto"`, `providerOrder: []`, `selectedPrimary: null`, `primaryApproved: false`, `exclusionReason: null`, and `availableKeys` with all false values). Real routing chains, strategies, and key presence are only served to verified owner sessions.
> - **`/api/core/state` GET:** replaced reliance on deployment-wide beta mode with authoritative session/role evaluation (`isPublicPreviewVisitor(session) || !isOwnerSession(session)`). Authenticated non-owners receive a truthful zero-state projection (`jobs: []`, `job: null`, `probes: []`, 0 MCP/models, unreachable vault) rather than leaking owner-global jobs, local service probes, and system topology.
> - **`/api/mcp/connect` GET (Adjacent):** closed non-owner read exposure. Authenticated non-owner sessions receive `emptyByoMcpResponse()` (`plugins: []`, `topology.nodes: []`, `topology.axons: []`) rather than running `listMcpServers()` and `generateDynamicTopology()`.
> - **`/api/vault/system` GET (Adjacent):** closed non-owner read exposure. Authenticated non-owner sessions receive unconfigured safe projection (`strategy: "auto"`, `models: []`, all `providers` configured=false and source="none", `routingChains: ROUTE_CHAINS`) rather than leaking owner-global models, routing strategy, or key flags.
> - **`/api/vault/system/n8n` & `/health` GET (Adjacent):** closed non-owner read exposure. Authenticated non-owner sessions receive empty host value (`host: { value: "", source: "none" }`, `apiKey: { configured: false, source: "none" }`). Health probe returns `status: "offline"`, `host: ""` without triggering outbound network probes to the owner's n8n server.
> - **`/api/vault/[agentId]` GET (Adjacent):** authenticated non-owners receive `{ providers: [] }`.
> - **`/api/telemetry` & `/api/spatial/telemetry` GET (Adjacent):** authenticated non-owners receive clean zero-state snapshots (`connectedMcpCount: 0`, `executionState: "idle"`, 0 active jobs).

> **2026-09-30, 18:42, Claude Code — Cloudflare Workers private-beta build path (vinext) + beta-store race fix. UNCOMMITTED, NOT DEPLOYED; stopped at the user's approval gate (no wrangler login / secrets / Google callback / deploy / commit / push).**
> - **Why:** user wants a $0 private beta on Cloudflare Workers alongside (not replacing) local `next dev` and Vercel. Cloudflare's docs (updated 2026-08-25) name **vinext** the recommended Next.js 16 path; OpenNext still can't run Node middleware, and Next 16 `proxy.ts` is always Node middleware, so OpenNext would have meant rewriting `proxy.ts` (rejected by user).
> - **Setup:** `vinext init` in stable "legacy wrangler" mode (the default mode pulls `@cloudflare/vite-plugin@2.0.0-beta.sha-*` + the `cf` CLI; rejected as pre-release). New: `wrangler.jsonc` (worker `growforge-beta`, `nodejs_compat`, workers.dev, `BETA_MODE=true` var), `vite.config.mts`, `cloudflare-env.d.ts`, `.dev.vars.example`; `package.json` gained `cf:dev|cf:build|cf:preview|cf:types|cf:deploy`, dev deps, `vinext`, `react-server-dom-webpack@19.2.8` (pinned — latest wants React 19.3), and **`"type": "module"`** (required by vinext). `next dev`, `next build` (full production build re-run, 39 pages) and the auto-start dev server were re-verified unaffected. `next.config.ts` and `auth.ts` did NOT need changes. Backups: scratchpad `cf-backup-20260930-175515`.
> - **Found and fixed during setup (all build-output only, in `vite.config.mts`):** (1) Tailwind v4 `@import` unresolved by Vite's postcss-import → `@tailwindcss/vite`; (2) **Vite copies `public/uploads` (owner avatar/cover/logo) and `public/generated` into Workers static assets, which are served BEFORE the Worker/proxy runs → would be public**; stripped at build; (3) **`@cloudflare/vite-plugin` writes `.env.local` (AUTH_SECRET, VAULT_MASTER_KEY, N8N_API_KEY, HF_CREDENTIALS…) into `dist/server/.dev.vars`** — not uploaded by `wrangler deploy` and gitignored, but it would make local "beta" previews run with real keys; deleted at build, previews use an explicit beta-only env file; (4) `pkce-challenge` (MCP SDK) breaks `cf:dev` pre-bundling → excluded.
> - **Auth.js under vinext — works.** `vinext check` flags next-auth as incompatible; tested instead of trusting it. The one failure seen was a **local-http artifact, not an incompatibility**: in `proxy.ts`, next-auth rebuilds the session URL as `https://` (vinext sets no `x-forwarded-proto`), so over plain-http preview it looked for `__Secure-authjs.session-token` while the browser held `authjs.session-token` → every session null in the proxy only (`/api/auth/session` itself worked). Confirmed by adding `x-forwarded-proto: http` (→ 200), then by running the preview over **https** (as production is) — all green. Hence `cf:preview` uses `--local-protocol https`.
> - **Beta store race (Stage B, `src/lib/beta/store.ts`):** the Upstash backend was one JSON blob read-modify-written; the in-process queue can't serialise separate instances. Proven with 3 module instances vs one store (40 rounds): freeze undone by concurrent sign-in 39/40, deleted tester resurrected + block lost + data kept 40/40, concurrent invite lost 40/40. Rewritten: per-record keys under `growforge:beta:v2:` and every check-then-write as one atomic Lua `EVAL`; 60 rounds → 0 anomalies. One-time atomic import of the old `growforge:beta:v1` document (single winner via `SET NX`, others wait then fail closed; old key kept for rollback) — verified. Fails closed on Workers without Upstash (never a Worker-local file) — verified live. File backend (local dev) behaviour unchanged. Emulator note: ioredis-mock's Lua drops dynamically built tables, so `LUA_DELETE` returns a literal `{0/1,…}` (identical on real Redis).
> - **Verified (Workers runtime = `wrangler dev` on the production build, https, Upstash-REST emulator over ioredis-mock in scratchpad):** existing `verify_beta_access.mjs` 27/27 (scratch copy: cookie name + backend expectation parametrised) before and after the store rewrite; `test-beta-signin.mts` 13/13 on file and on Upstash; HTTP-level concurrent freeze/delete vs tester writes 15/15; CORE + Explore render with WebGL, 0 console errors, beta badge (headless Chrome, tester cookie); no `.env.local` secret in any build file; tsc 0 errors; eslint clean.
> - **Size / Free tier:** compressed upload **1,461.66 KiB** (Free limit 3 MiB). ~560 KiB of it is code testers can't reach (attachments/pdf-parse+mammoth 265, router 127, MCP client 108, orchestrator 58) — optional trim. **CPU:** local workerd reports cpu_time 0, so measured warm latency minus a 5–16 ms static-asset baseline: APIs/auth ≈0–10 ms, full SSR `/` and `/login` ≈35–60 ms → page renders very likely exceed Free's 10 ms CPU; only a real workers.dev deploy can settle it (Free never bills; overage = error 1102).

> **2026-09-30, Claude Code — PRIVATE BETA auth + tester management, UNCOMMITTED, NOT DEPLOYED; local production-build verified.** Enabled by env `BETA_MODE=true` (off = previous behaviour unchanged). > **2026-09-29 15:01 +06:00, Claude Code — arbor test (verify_brain03_arbor.mjs) brought in line with the current cord design; whole suite PASSES; TEST-ONLY change, no app code touched in this step, UNCOMMITTED.** Follow-up to the record-clarity entry: the data-pulse check failed because it measured cords at ARRIVAL, where the current design hides them (cord shader life *= smoothstep(0.45,0.78,journeyDepth) x nearest-endpoint reveal), and because it force-enabled packets with the `setPulseGain(1)` hook — but packets are never attached to a cord in production (`pulseMember` is never set; `aFocus` sums to 0 at every depth; probed at d=200/150/110/80). So that check was testing a feature that was deliberately removed. Replaced with (c1) IDLE PLASMA at d~110 (journeyDepth 0.92, nearest endpoint ~65): cords add ~46 luminance over the background (A/B by hiding the link material, repeatable), the plasma breathes (std ~10), and it is a standing texture, not directional travel — "directional" = every point-pair along the cord peaks strongly at a non-zero lag of the SAME sign; measured pairs are in phase (0,3: lag 0 r .82; 2,5: lag 0 r .94) or scatter in sign (-0.62 / +0.68 / +0.66) = drift noise. (c2) DATA PACKETS only for real recorded events: idle cords carry none (gain 0, member -1, focus 0), and forcing the gain hook without an event attaches no packet and adds no light (interleaved on/off windows, one-sided ratio < 1.10). No traffic is fabricated; the only synthetic signal is an in-script self-check that the lag detector flags a travelling wave and not an in-phase one. NOTE: there is currently NO production source of recorded edge events, so "a real event produces a packet" cannot be tested yet — when one is wired, add that positive test. Genuine test bugs found and fixed on the way: (1) the old lag detector let sparse-overlap lags win (corr > 1 at +-2.4 s) — now lags limited to +-1.6 s and >=70% overlap required; (2) hard-coded category index 3 for the MCP layer — MCP is now index 2 (category order changed); the test looks it up by id and checks exactly that layer is off and the rest on, before and after refreshes (the FILTER WORKED, only the test was stale); (3) hard-coded chip count 6 — the UI has 5 category chips; now asserts one chip per data category. No thresholds lowered (discoverability stays 1.35). Results: arbor PASS, lifecycle PASS, node_release PASS, drag_selection PASS, tsc clean. Knobs (top of `SpatialCanvas.tsx`): `AMBIENT_EXTRA_STARS/DUST/GOLD` (density), `AMBIENT_OPACITY_SCALE` (brightness), `AMBIENT_FADE_START_DIST/END_DIST` (where it fades). > **BRAIN SCROLL-JOURNEY DEPTH FIX (2026-09-29, 04:56, Claude Code). Working branch `review/brain02-source-audit-20260927`. UNCOMMITTED.**
>
> User: "the BRAIN scroll journey is still wrong... from the second screenshot onward, scrolling immediately brings me into MISSIONS. Remember our original vision: I want a MUCH longer, immersive inward journey through BRAIN before reaching MISSIONS." Also required: manual scroll must not prematurely trigger the MISSIONS HUD/rails; nav-click MISSIONS must keep its own quicker guided transition; only the very end of the journey should be a deliberate cinematic pass-through.
>
> - **Root cause, found by reading the tier-resolution code, not guessing.** The per-frame surface-tier block in `SpatialCanvas.tsx` mapped camera distance to tier: `d>660` -> CORE, `240<d<=660` -> BRAIN, `d<=240` -> MISSIONS (`setVisualMode("missions")`), unconditionally, on EVERY frame, for BOTH manual scroll and guided navigation. BRAIN's knowledge layer (labels/hover/click) is gated purely on `visualModeRef.current === "brain"` (`SpatialCanvas.tsx`'s `knowledgeTarget`). So the instant manual scroll crossed d=240 — a few notches past BRAIN's own arrival distance of 330 — the surface silently flipped to MISSIONS and the knowledge layer died, long before the camera was anywhere near the nucleus. That is the "immediately brings me into MISSIONS" the user described.
> - **Fix.** Replaced the `240` cutoff with `BRAIN_DEEP_PASSTHROUGH_DIST = 95` — just above where the existing guided dive already rests (`MISSIONS_CAMERA_DIST = 90`). BRAIN now stays the active surface, fully interactive, all the way from arrival (330) down to 95. Crossing 95 does **not** flip the surface directly: it calls `handleEnterCore()` — the exact same cinematic dive already used by the "Missions" nav button (FOV warp, roll, exposure ramp) — so the final transition is the deliberate pass-through the user asked for, not an abrupt swap, and nav-click MISSIONS is completely unaffected (still its own quicker guided path, untouched).
> - **Verified with a live, single-notch scroll sweep** (not just code reading): from d=291.6 down to d=96.8, `brainInteractive` stayed `true` and all 28 record labels stayed visible on **every single sample**. At the very next notch (d=94.6) `brainInteractive` flipped to `false` and camera `fov` began climbing (48.0 -> 48.1) in the same frame — confirming the boundary triggers cleanly exactly once, right at the threshold, with no earlier or later drift.
> - **Screenshots** (`growforge-ui/verification/s1/journey/`): `A_outer_brain.png` (arrival, matches the user's already-approved first screenshot), `B_mid_depth.png` (d≈185, all names revealed), `C_deep_particles.png` (d≈103, nucleus filling much more of the frame, still fully interactive — confirmed via probe), `D2_final_core_approach_pretrigger.png` (d=97.7, one notch from the boundary, still on BRAIN, `brainInteractive:true`), `E_transition_boundary.png` / `F_missions_arrived.png` (mid-dive and settled at MISSIONS).
> - **Also extended:** `BRAIN_JOURNEY_MS` was already 7600 (raised from 5800 in the prior corridor-removal pass); left as is since the manual journey's new depth is the change that actually mattered here — the guided nav-click duration was never the reported problem.

> **S1 VISUAL CORRECTION — MISSIONS EXPOSURE + COMPOSITION (2026-09-29, 03:58, Claude Code). UNCOMMITTED.**
>
> Rejection was: overexposed nucleus, messy particle cluster, disconnected-looking lines.
>
> - **Measured the blowout instead of guessing.** A horizontal luminance slice through the core at MISSIONS read **mean 253-255 over a ~200px span** (every pixel clipped) and then collapsed to mean 2-20 past ~200px. No midtones anywhere — which is exactly why the corridor read as disconnected specks floating in black.
> - **A false lead, recorded so nobody repeats it.** I first swept `uBrightness` on the nucleus and membrane at runtime and saw *no change at any value down to 0.15x*. That was a broken experiment, not a finding: the engine's animate loop reassigns those uniforms every frame, so the sweep was overwritten before the next paint. Runtime sweeps are only valid for uniforms the loop does not drive.
> - **Actual cause.** `NeutronCoreShader` sizes membrane points by `320.0 / -mvPosition.z`. MISSIONS sits *inside* the envelope at d=89, so nearby particles inflate to the 48px clamp and thousands of them stack under additive blending into a solid white disc. The corona sprites were NOT the cause — they were already at opacity 0.024/0.019 from the earlier BRAIN damping.
> - **Fix:** a `uMissionCalm` uniform shared by reference between the membrane and nucleus materials (0 on CORE/BRAIN, so both are untouched): sprite size -30%, size clamp 48px -> 21px, particle intensity -46%, nucleus body alpha -50%; plus `MISSION_EXPOSURE_DAMP` on `renderer.toneMappingExposure` for overall midtone range. Re-measured: clipped cells **5 -> 1**, shoulders 201/198 -> 34/36.
> - **Composition.** The corridor was spanning -330..+430px with a 96px rise, which floated the whole structure above and beside the nucleus. Re-centred to -150..+355 with a 62px rise so the run visibly emanates from the core: stages now project at x 637/775/903/1020/1128 with the nucleus at 800,450 — the core sits on the run. Spine and department link opacities roughly doubled, stage/department node sizes raised, so links terminate in visible nodes instead of fading to nothing. Atmosphere damping was eased back from the first pass (-58% -> -46% intensity) because it had stopped blowing out but also stopped reading as a volume.
> - **My own mistake, and the cost.** Several of these patches were applied with `python open(p,'w')`, which on Windows defaults to cp1252 and silently corrupted the em-dashes in `missionStyle.ts`. `tsc` passed (TypeScript tolerated it) but Turbopack refused the file — `invalid utf-8 sequence at index 3272` — and the dev server served **500 for several minutes**. Repaired and added a repo-wide UTF-8 integrity scan to the check list (now 0 bad files). **Always pass `encoding='utf-8'` when scripting file edits on this machine.**
> - **Preserved:** single renderer (1 canvas), all `/api/core/state` bindings, hub hover + click -> inspector focus, drag-does-not-select, stage chips, Depts/Inspector rails, header/nav/Assistant. A completed mission reports `activePulses: 0` — it looks finished, not busy.
> - **Checks:** tsc 0 - lint 0 - UTF-8 scan 0 bad - drag-selection PASSED (28/28 canonical records) - privacy isolation PASSED. Screenshots `verification/s1/10..13`.
> - **Not yet done:** queued user requests — inward-only scroll journey from CORE, softer core brightness on BRAIN with slow fade-in of data particles, inline sidebar search (drop the centre overlay), and a bright pulsing "signal" state for a searched node.

> **S1 — MISSIONS IN THE PERSISTENT SPATIAL SCENE (2026-09-28, 19:24, Claude Code). Working branch `review/brain02-source-audit-20260927`. UNCOMMITTED.**
>
> First milestone of the Continuous Spatial Experience phase. MISSIONS is now a perspective of the
> same universe as CORE and BRAIN instead of an opaque dashboard with a private WebGL scene.
>
> - **What was wrong.** `CoreZoomTier` mounted `CoreSphere3D`, which built its **own `THREE.WebGLRenderer` + `Scene`** and drew an unrelated decorative department sphere over a fully opaque `bg-[#050811]` panel. The real universe kept rendering, invisible, underneath — two live WebGL contexts, one of them hidden. Measured before the change: **2 canvases**.
>
> - **What replaced it.** `spatial/missions/MissionField.ts` (new) — a layer of the ONE scene, with the same lifecycle contract as `BrainField`: a smoothed 0..1 gate owned by `SpatialCanvas` (`missionGateRef`) drives visibility and interactivity together. One hub per real department of the selected job, orbiting the **real nucleus**; link from just outside the nucleus surface to each hub; colour, weight, link brightness and size all from the department's actual step status. The travelling pulse runs **only** while a department's own step is genuinely `active`, exactly the rule CoreSphere3D used — with nothing running the ring is still. Hub structure reuses BRAIN's record language (tight specular core + fine rim) so it reads as the same universe.
>
> - **A wrong turn, caught by measurement.** The first revision spread hubs over a 3D fibonacci shell scaled to the nucleus radius, the way CoreSphere3D did. Projected, that bunched them unevenly — measured **30px to 148px** from the core, one hub almost on top of the nucleus — and buried them in the membrane dust. Replaced with a dial: hubs sit on a ring in the camera's own plane (slight tilt so it still belongs to a 3D world), and the ring's world radius is solved per frame from a target **screen** radius, so it holds the same readable size at any camera distance. Re-measured: all 8 hubs at **197-209px**, evenly spaced.
>
> - **Second wrong turn, also caught.** With the ring rendering, hover and click did nothing: the MISSIONS overlay (`pointer-events-auto`, full viewport) swallowed every pointer event before the canvas saw it. Fixed by inverting the default — the overlay wrapper and `CoreZoomTier`'s root are now `pointer-events-none` and each instrumentation panel opts back in individually, so the gap around the core is a real window onto the scene. Verified: `document.elementFromPoint` at a hub returns `CANVAS`, and wheel-scrolling over a panel still scrolls the surface (scrollTop 0 -> 400).
>
> - **Third, subtler bug.** The BRAIN animate path was force-clearing the shared hover card every frame whenever BRAIN's own layer was not pickable, which wiped the MISSIONS department readout the instant it was painted (card content correct, opacity stuck at 0). Both clear sites now also check `missions.isPickable()`.
>
> - **Preserved, verified against source:** every `/api/core/state` field CoreZoomTier showed — job title/brief/percent/stage/elapsed, per-department assignment, task, step status and percent, the project selector, test-run filter, New Brief launcher, cost/token usage, Live Activity, approvals. Clicking a hub selects the same department the left card would, and only when that department is actually `assigned` (measured: focus moved "Strategy & Intelligence" -> "Finance & Operations"). Drag across a hub orbits and does **not** select, matching BRAIN-04A's press-latch rule. Hover shows the department's real name and `done · 100%` from the source, nothing inferred.
>
> - **Retired, not deleted.** `CoreSphere3D.tsx` is untouched on disk and no longer imported anywhere; only two explanatory comments reference it by name. Per instruction it stays until S2 signs off.
>
> - **Files:** `spatial/missions/MissionField.ts` (new), `spatial/missions/missionStyle.ts` (new, all tunables centralised for later theming), `spatial/SpatialCanvas.tsx`, `spatial/CoreZoomTier.tsx`, `app/globals.css` (`.missions-scrim` — darkens the outer thirds where the panel columns sit, stays clear through the middle).
>
> - **Verification:** `tsc` 0 · `lint` 0 · `build` 35/35 routes · **drag-selection PASSED** (3 drag, 3 click, 28/28 canonical records) · **lifecycle PASSED** (22 steps, 4 cycles, 28/28 deep-zoom names, pivot glide monotonic) · **depth PASSED** · **privacy isolation PASSED (13/13)**. Full nav cycle CORE -> BRAIN -> MISSIONS -> BRAIN -> CORE: **1 canvas at every step**, MISSIONS gate 1 only at d=89 and 0 elsewhere, BRAIN interactive only at d=330, navigation visible throughout, **zero console errors**, and a BRAIN record still opens correctly afterwards. Public preview (`PUBLIC_PREVIEW_MODE=true`, fresh production build on :3006): **0 hubs**, 0 records, empty state shown, no hover card, 5 clicks in the core window opened nothing — **no sample departments injected**. Screenshots in `growforge-ui/verification/s1/`.
>

Confirm this is wanted. (b) Only the `done` status has been seen live, because the local job history is complete — `pending`/`active`/`error`/`unassigned` colours and the travelling pulse are implemented from the same source fields but have not been observed against a running job. (c) MISSIONS is still a single-column stack below `lg`, where the scrim goes flat and covers the core; the mobile layout has not been redesigned. (d) SYSTEMS, Nora's cross-layer persistence and the department -> agent -> tool hierarchy are untouched, as instructed.

> **BRAIN-04B — LIVING SEMANTIC PARTICLE DISCOVERABILITY (2026-09-28, 18:21, Claude Code). Working branch `review/brain02-source-audit-20260927`. UNCOMMITTED.**
>
> **Headline: the arbor discoverability assertion still fails (0.89 vs its 1.35 threshold), and that is the correct outcome. The metric is confounded and cannot be moved by any legitimate treatment of the records. Evidence below. The visual work was done anyway, because the diagnosis exposed a real problem the metric does not measure.**
>
> - **Method.** Rebuilt the arbor suite's exact probe (14px peak / 64px surround, median of 6 frames) in the same headless SwiftShader configuration, but kept the two terms separate instead of collapsing them to one ratio, and tagged every sample with its world radius and its SCREEN distance from the projected nucleus. Diagnostic archived at `growforge-ui/verification/brain04b/diag_discoverability.mjs` (gitignored).
>
> - **Finding 1 — the numerator is saturated, so records have no headroom.** Record peak luminance is **255 in every radius band** (min 209-232); ordinary particles sit at 83-146. Records are already ~2x brighter at their centre than the dust, and clipped against the 8-bit readback ceiling. Confirmed by direct experiment: cranking record rim + halo energy ~8x past anything tasteful moved the ratio from 1.02 to **1.03**, left peak14 at 255 and mean64 at 16.69. **Nothing done to the record sprite can change this number.** A future session that "fixes" this metric by restyling the records is measuring noise.
>
> - **Finding 2 — the ratio is Simpson's paradox, not a discoverability result.** Comparing the two populations *at matched screen distance from the nucleus*, records beat ordinary particles in **every single band**:
>   | screen dist from nucleus | records | ordinary | band ratio |
>   |---|---|---|---|
>   | 0-90px | 2.5 | 1.6 | **1.60** |
>   | 90-150px | 11.8 | 6.2 | **1.91** |
>   | 150-220px | 25.2 | 16.0 | **1.57** |
>   | 220-320px | 32.8 | 18.7 | **1.76** |
>   | 320-900px | 47.4 | 16.3 | **2.91** |
>   Every band clears 1.35. The aggregate is 0.89-1.13 only because the two populations are drawn from different parts of the screen: records project at a **median 140px** from the nucleus (they are carried by the hub's nearest neighbours, which is the approved design), ordinary samples at a **median 257px**. Local background mean runs ~100-160 beside the nucleus versus ~3-5 in the outskirts, so the record median is computed mostly from records sitting in the core's glare and the ordinary median mostly from particles in the dark. The headline number reverses the per-band truth.
>
> - **Finding 3 — a hypothesis the measurement killed.** I expected records to be inflating each other's surround (carriers are deliberately clustered and their key filaments deliberately brighter). Switching the entire semantic layer off and re-probing the *same boxes* moved the surround from 18.57 to 18.94 — **~0%**. Records contribute essentially nothing to their own measured background. Dropped.
>
> - **Finding 4 — why reducing the background cannot help either.** After the corona change the arbor's real contrast rose 12.85 -> 13.14 **and** its ordinary contrast rose 14.32 -> 14.75. A uniform reduction in background lifts both populations' peak/surround roughly proportionally, so a *ratio* of contrasts is invariant to it. Combined with Finding 1, the only routes left to 1.35 are: dim the ordinary atmosphere (guts the approved look, and is exactly the gaming the brief forbids), move records off their real carriers to quieter positions (violates requirement 1), or de-confound the sampling (a change to the test, which this task forbids). **Recommended follow-up: match the two populations by screen distance from the nucleus before taking medians — the assertion would then measure what it intends to, and the current code already passes it in every band.**
>
> - **What the diagnosis DID expose, and what was fixed.** Roughly half the records project within ~150px of the nucleus, where the two corona sprites washed the local background to a mean of 40-110. Those records were genuinely hard to see — a real user-facing problem the ratio does not capture. Changes:
>   1. `neutronCore/NeutronCoreEngine.ts` — the inner corona is eased back 34%, the outer wash 52% and pulled 14% inward **as the atmosphere connects into BRAIN**. Driven by `BrainDrive.links`, which is 0 on CORE, so the approved CORE look is untouched. Follows the precedent already in that function (`uBrightness * (1 - 0.14 * brain.links)`).
>   2. `brain/BrainField.ts` RECORD_FRAG — tighter specular core (gaussian 10 -> 15), the wide smear cut back, plus a fine importance-scaled structural **rim**. Against a background already clipped to white the only cue left is high-frequency structure, which a smooth bloom has none of.
>   3. Glare response: the vertex shader computes each record's NDC distance from the projected origin and passes it as `vGlare`; records silhouetted against the nucleus withdraw their soft halo (which only adds to the wash they compete with) and tighten + strengthen their rim instead. **This measurably does not move the metric** — a pure visual gain, and the clearest single example of the metric/quality conflict.
>   4. `brain/semanticStyle.ts` (new) — every colour, radius, falloff and damping constant in one place (requirement 9), each carrying the measurement that justifies it.
>
> - **Preserved, verified:** single shared particle population and the CORE/BRAIN motion equations untouched; records still ride their real carriers (28/28 distinct, 0 unassigned, stable across 4 BRAIN cycles); BRAIN-04A screen-space hit testing, canonical-id mapping, press latching, holographic inspection and label placement unchanged; no new mesh, sphere, badge or second graph system; dark white-cyan language intact.
>
> - **Verification run:** `tsc` 0; `lint` 0; `build` 35/35 routes. `verify_brain_drag_selection.mjs` **PASSED** (3 drag cases, 3 click cases, 28/28 canonical records). `verify_brain_lifecycle.mjs` **PASSED** (22 steps, 28/28 names at deep zoom, pivot glide monotonic). `verify_brain_depth.mjs` **PASSED**. `scripts/test-preview-isolation.ts` **PASSED** (13/13). `verify_brain03_arbor.mjs` **FAILS on the discoverability assertion only** (0.89); every other assertion in that suite passes. Public preview re-checked in-browser with the new shaders: 0 records, 0 labels, no hover card, 5 clicks opened nothing — **no sample nodes injected**. Screenshots in `growforge-ui/verification/brain04b/` (`before_*` / `after_*` at matched camera distance and matched hovered record; the "before" set was produced by neutralising the new tunables at source rather than reverting files, so the sets differ only in these constants).
>
> - **Remaining issues / honest limits.** The arbor assertion is red and will stay red until the sampling is matched — do not let a future session turn it green by dimming the atmosphere. The metric is also harness-sensitive: my probe reports 1.02-1.13 and the arbor suite 0.89 on identical code, because the two arrive at BRAIN on different camera azimuths and so measure different records against the glare. Records that project directly onto the nucleus disc top out near contrast 2.5 whatever is done to them — they are occluded by the brightest object in the scene, and only camera movement reveals them. Reduced-motion in BRAIN, mobile/narrow layouts and lower quality tiers remain untested (unchanged from BRAIN-04A).

> **BRAIN-04A DRAG-RELEASE CORRECTION (2026-09-28, 17:44, Codex). UNCOMMITTED.** `SpatialCanvas.tsx` now treats an existing pointerdown as authoritative: movement over 5px always cancels selection, while an in-place click uses the canonical record captured on pointerdown even if the field moves or the original target was empty. Keyboard/synthetic clicks without pointerdown resolve at their click position. Added `scripts/verify_brain_drag_selection.mjs` with live browser regressions for empty-to-node, node-to-empty and node-to-node drags, synthetic and timed moving-target clicks, and all 28 canonical records. TypeScript, browser regression, and dual-environment privacy isolation passed. No commit, push, or deployment.

> **BRAIN-04A — RELIABLE NODE INTERACTION & HOLOGRAPHIC INSPECTION (2026-09-28, 16:56, Claude Code). Working branch `review/brain02-source-audit-20260927`. UNCOMMITTED — left in the working tree for independent review, per instruction.**
>
> - **The reported bug:** node names near the bottom of the screen (and sometimes elsewhere) could not be clicked.
>
> - **What was actually measured (not assumed).** Drove the live local BRAIN through Playwright, swept the cursor over a grid to reveal every name, captured each name plate's real `getBoundingClientRect()`, then clicked (a) the centre of the name and (b) the projected particle, reading back which record the reader panel opened. Baseline, 22 records:
>   - Clicking the **name**: 10/22 opened anything at all; **0/22 opened the record the name belonged to** (e.g. "Google Gemini" opened Meta Ads Department, "GitHub" opened HubSpot, "Apollo.io" opened Vercel).
>   - Clicking the **particle**: 20/22 opened something, but **9/22 opened the wrong record** in the dense hub cluster (y≈355–390).
>   So the bug was far broader than "the bottom of the screen": *names were never clickable anywhere*, and the dot was ~50% wrong wherever records clustered. The "bottom of the screen" perception comes from the sparse lower region, where names are long and sit furthest from the only real target.
>
> - **Root cause (two mechanisms, both confirmed in source and in the browser):**
>   1. **Names were not click targets at all.** The label layer (`SpatialCanvas.tsx`) is `pointer-events-none` and every label element also set `pointer-events:none` inline. A click on a name fell through to the canvas, which raycast **at the cursor** against invisible `SphereGeometry(11)` pick meshes sitting at each record's own position. A plate is drawn at `+9px, −14px` from its particle and runs up to ~200px wide, so a click on the name raycast into empty space (nothing opened) or into a *neighbouring* record's sphere (the wrong record opened).
>   2. **World-space pick spheres, not screen-space targets.** A fixed 11-unit radius means a far-side record's target is ~40% smaller in pixels than a near one, and overlapping spheres around the hub resolved by "first sphere the ray entered", which is not the record under the cursor.
>
> - **A hypothesis that the measurement killed.** Before measuring I expected a third cause: that `pickEnabled = drive.links > 0.6` gated picking off while labels (`brainReveal ≥ 0.72`) were already fading in, leaving a window of visible-but-dead names. Working the easing back through `driveFromReveal` shows `links ≈ 0.82` at the label threshold — picking turns on *before* names appear, so it is the wider gate. Not a defect; dropped.
>
> - **The fix — one screen-space hit test, shared by hover and click.**
>   - `BrainField.ts`: the invisible pick meshes and their geometry/material are **deleted**. Keeping them would have left two independently-gated notions of "what is clickable" that had already drifted apart. `BrainLabelData` now carries the canonical `node` and real `degree`; `isPickable()` exposes the single interactivity gate.
>   - `SpatialCanvas.tsx`: the animate loop publishes `hitTargetsRef` — every interactive record's current screen footprint (projected dot + its name plate's box), built from **the exact projection that positions the labels**, so what is drawn and what is hittable cannot diverge. `pickAtPixel()` resolves a name plate first (that is what the user is aiming at), else the nearest dot within 15px. Hover and click call the same resolver.
>   - Label **de-clutter by displacement**. Around the hub a dozen plates land on the same few hundred pixels; whichever loses the hit test is drawn but permanently unclickable — the same defect as a name with no target.
>
> - **Two wrong turns taken and corrected, both caught by measurement:**
>   1. *De-clutter by suppression* (hide plates buried >30%). Clicks became 24/24 correct, but `verify_brain_lifecycle.mjs` step 20 failed — only 22/28 names soft-appeared at deep zoom, breaking the BRAIN-03g contract that *every* name appears. Replaced with displacement (nudge colliding plates clear in row-height steps): 28/28 names visible, **0 overlapping pairs**.
>   2. *Ranking the layout by alpha, with the hovered plate first.* Hovering then re-ran the layout and slid the plate out from under the pointer — it highlighted on hover and missed on click (measured: 15/26). Fixed by making the arrangement **cursor-independent**: ranked only by importance then id, and run over *every* on-screen plate whether faded in or not, so a name lighting up never moves its neighbours.
>
> - **Press-latch.** The field never stops moving, so a record can drift off the cursor between mousedown and mouseup. `pointerdown` latches the target; `click` uses it if the pointer moved ≤5px (a larger move is an orbit drag, not a selection). This closed the last measured hover-hit/click-miss case.
>
> - **Camera (explicit requirement: no snapping/teleporting).** Sidebar/search selection previously did `controls.target.set(...)` — a whole-record-radius jump in one frame. Now `pivotFocusRef` eases the pivot (exponential, tau 0.45), dolly distance untouched so selecting can never cross a tier boundary. This initially **broke** the lifecycle suite's "pivot moves monotonically toward the nucleus" assertion, because the focus glide was still easing outward while the return-home glide started. Fixed by giving the pivot exactly one authority at a time: the focus glide is released on user wheel, on orbit start, on closing inspection, and on leaving BRAIN. Result: `nonIncreasing:true`, 118 intermediate frames, max step 2.1 — a real glide where there used to be a snap.
>
> - **Inspection UX.** `brain/inspectionTheme.ts` (new) centralises every surface/border/glow/type token for both surfaces so the theme can be retuned without touching interaction code. Hover shows a contextual card with **real** category, title and recorded link count — no invented status. Click opens the existing `NoteReaderModal` (the working data flow was reused, not replaced) restyled from those tokens, with an opaque-enough plate to hold small type over the moving field, `aria-label` on close, Escape-to-close, and `motion-reduce` on its entrance. The hover card is cleared whenever the knowledge layer stops being interactive — a regression this session introduced (the card stayed visible over CORE) and then fixed.
>
> - **Identity.** Selection resolves through the canonical record id carried on the anchor. No display-name lookup anywhere.
>
> - **Files changed:** `src/components/spatial/SpatialCanvas.tsx`, `src/components/spatial/brain/BrainField.ts`, `src/components/spatial/NoteReaderModal.tsx`, `src/components/spatial/brain/inspectionTheme.ts` (new), `src/app/globals.css` (hover-card type ramp).
>
> - **Verification actually run:**
>   - `npx tsc --noEmit` 0 errors; `npm run lint` 0 errors; `npm run build` compiled, 35/35 routes prerendered.
>   - `scripts/verify_brain_lifecycle.mjs` **PASSED** (22 steps, 4 BRAIN cycles, mapping stable at 28 records across all four, 28/28 names at deep zoom, pivot glide monotonic, no console errors).
>   - `scripts/verify_brain_depth.mjs` **PASSED**.
>   - Browser grid sweep, 418 cursor positions across the full viewport (top, bottom, both sides): 33 landed on a record — **33/33 hover-title == opened-record**, 33/33 opened, 33/33 showed the pointer cursor; the other 385 hovered nothing and opened nothing.
>   - Named-plate pass, 26 plates spanning y 111–686: 23 opened, 22 exact, 25/26 hover==click; the handful of misses were the harness clicking stale coordinates (the scene drifts between sampling and clicking) — hover and click agreed with each other in every such case, which is the invariant that matters.
>   - **Public preview** (`PUBLIC_PREVIEW_MODE=true`, `next start -p 3005`): `/api/spatial/graph` returns `{nodes:[],links:[],categories:[]}`, user `Operator`; BRAIN shows 0 records and 0 labels, six clicks across the field opened nothing, hover card never appeared. **No illustrative nodes were injected to demonstrate interaction.**
>   - **CORE → BRAIN → CORE**: on CORE the label layer is `visibility:hidden`, `interactive:false`, hover card opacity 0, and a click in the field opens nothing; returning to BRAIN restores `interactive:true` and labels. No residual interactive overlay.
>
> - **Pre-existing failure, NOT caused by this work — flagged for the roadmap.** `scripts/verify_brain03_arbor.mjs` fails its "real records are measurably more distinguishable than ordinary particles" assertion (ratio 0.89–0.90, needs >1). I confirmed this is pre-existing by stashing every change in this session and re-running on the untouched tree: **identical ratio 0.89**. So the record particles are currently *less* visually distinct than ordinary atmosphere particles, and earlier notes claiming this suite passes are stale. Worth its own task — it is a discoverability problem, not an interaction one, and this milestone's screen-space picking makes it less painful but does not address it.
>
> - **Known limits, untested:** reduced-motion inside BRAIN, mobile/narrow layouts, lower quality tiers. The inspection panel is still the fixed right-hand `max-w-md` dock — it has not been given a mobile bottom-sheet form.
>

> **GITHUB/VERCEL RECONCILIATION + scratch-artifact cleanup (2026-09-28, 11:03, Claude Code). Committed `781c4b6` + `8e59960`, pushed. Production deliberately NOT touched.**
> - **User request:** "update on github and vercel", then — after checking Vercel — a bug report: the deployed header showed `CORE → Missions → Brain → Systems`, but the current design is `CORE → Brain → Missions → Systems`. Asked whether GitHub/Vercel actually had the latest local work, and to fix it if not.
> - **First (wrong) hypothesis:** that the push had silently failed or that Vercel production tracked `master`, which was 12 commits stale — so production was simply showing old code. The staleness is real (see below) but it was NOT what the user was looking at: the Sept-24 `master` build (`670ec5b`) has a completely different `SpatialHud.tsx` ("Home Assistant Core", "AI Brain Knowledge Graph", pink-400 Brain icon, no `NAV_ITEMS` array at all), which does not match the screenshot's modern pill nav. Chasing the master-staleness answer alone would have "fixed" the wrong thing.
> - **Actual root cause, identified by differencing the nav array across commits:** `a90ba4c` = `CORE → Missions → Brain → Systems` + logo subtitle `AI OS`; `c41a6e8`/`781c4b6` = `CORE → Brain → Missions → Systems` + subtitle `Operating Ecosystem`. The screenshot matched `a90ba4c` on **both** signals. `a90ba4c` is the tip of the *other* branch, `review/core-ui-checkpoint-20260926`. Confirmed against the Vercel API: deployments `dpl_CeLnHS…`/`dpl_3qwGqy…` are `a90ba4c` previews from Sept 26. **The user was on a stale preview URL, not production and not the new preview.** The nav reorder itself was never broken — it landed in BRAIN-01 and has been correct in every commit since `c41a6e8`.
> - **Nothing was missing from GitHub.** `781c4b6` pushed cleanly; `origin/review/brain02-source-audit-20260927` was verified to contain all BRAIN-03 sources (`brain/BrainField.ts`, `brain/coreLightning.ts`, `neutronCore/coreParticleField.ts`, …) and the correct nav order. The matching preview (`growforge-digital-ai-5f0rc00i2-…`) built READY with the current code.
> - **Second, separate finding — Vercel production is 4 days stale.** Production target is pinned to `master @ 670ec5b` (2026-09-24, `dpl_8B24Wr94…`). `origin/master` was never advanced past that; it is **12 commits behind** the review branch. Production has therefore never served *any* of the CORE command-center or BRAIN work. **Left as-is on explicit user decision** ("hold — review preview first"): no merge to `master`, no production deploy, no change to the Vercel production-branch setting. This remains open and is the single reason the production domain does not match local.
> - **My own error, found while auditing the push:** the BRAIN-03g commit was staged with `git add -A`, which swept in three files the 2026-09-27 16:05 "SAFE GITHUB SOURCE SNAPSHOT" entry had listed **by name** as deliberately excluded private scratch artifacts — `.freebuff/project-id` (a UUID), `g1.png` (1.1 MB), `growforge-ui/header_calibrated_after.png` — plus `growforge-ui/verification/` (446 files, ~52 MB of browser capture `.jpg` + `summary.json`). None are `.env` files or credentials, but the exclusion was a stated policy and **nothing in `.gitignore` enforced it**, so a broad add silently overrode it. That gap was the real defect, not the single bad command.
> - **Fix (`8e59960`), shaped by user choice:** offered history rewrite + force-push vs. a forward-only commit; user chose **forward-only** ("remove in a new commit") — so the blobs stay reachable from `781c4b6` by design, avoiding a force-push on a branch that exists for independent third-party review. Offered keep/partial/drop for the verification captures; user chose **drop all + gitignore**. Implemented with `git rm --cached -r` so all four paths remain on disk locally (the evidence is not lost, only untracked), plus `.gitignore` rules with comments explaining *why* each is excluded so the next broad add cannot re-add them.
> - **Verified:** staged diff was exactly 449 deletions + 1 modification (`.gitignore`), with `git diff --cached --name-only | grep src/` returning **0** — no source file touched. After push + `git fetch`, `git ls-tree -r origin/review/…` returns **no** match for `freebuff|g1.png|header_calibrated|verification/`, while all nine BRAIN-03 spatial sources are still present and the remote nav order reads `core, brain, missions, systems`. > **BRAIN-03f: stability + verification pass, then BRAIN depth pass (2026-09-28, 09:51, Claude Code). Committed in `781c4b6`, pushed to `review/brain02-source-audit-20260927` (2026-09-28, 10:48).**
> - **Why:** independent read-only reconciliation audit of 03c/d/e (09:30) found: (D1) records re-assigned to *different* particles on every BRAIN entry — `BrainField.update` reset `assigned` whenever the field went inactive and re-picked a hub's linked records as nearest neighbours of *live* positions; (D5) manual scroll-out after a search/note focus left the orbit pivot on the record (only guided travel restored it) -> off-centre CORE; test gaps: the mid-reverse-journey step (the reported bug scenario) had no assertion, SYSTEMS was only opened from Missions, no cross-cycle mapping check; stale `state.md` header.
> - **Fix D1 (`brain/BrainField.ts`):** carriers are assigned once per graph topology (in `setKnowledge`), never on navigation. Linked records pick the hub's nearest neighbours in each particle's *rest frame* (base position rotated by its own fixed phase `rx*2PI`, the time-independent part of `coreParticleWorld`) -> a pure function of the shared particle buffer, independent of entry time. On a genuine topology change a record keeps its particle while its role (hub / which hub) is unchanged (`slot.role`); only new/changed records are placed. `debugInfo().assignmentRuns` exposes how often assignment ran. Shared particle architecture unchanged (members are still the same CORE buffer indices; no new population).
> - **Fix D5 (`SpatialCanvas.tsx`):** when BRAIN is left without a guided journey and the pivot is off the nucleus, `pivotReturnRef` starts a frame-rate-independent exponential glide of `controls.target` to the origin (tau 0.3 s) that ends once it arrives (normal user panning in CORE is not fought). Guided journeys keep the existing `travel.startTarget` easing. Also: the knowledge gate now closes while the Systems panel is open (`useAppState().isSettingsOpen`), so no BRAIN labels/hover/pick targets remain active under it; closing Systems restores BRAIN with the same pivot (no pivot return for that case).
> - **Tests (`scripts/verify_brain_lifecycle.mjs`, rewritten scenario, 4 BRAIN cycles, hard assertions everywhere):** Systems opened directly over BRAIN with a hovered+selected record; 5 mid-flight samples of a reverse BRAIN->CORE journey with a selected record (asserts CORE was selected while d<600); hover-only exit via Missions; search-focused record + manual wheel scroll-out (pivot glide must have >=8 intermediate frames, max per-frame step <20 %, monotonic; the record's old screen position must not be clickable); identical record->particle mapping and a single assignment run across all 4 cycles; centred target on every CORE/Missions return; shared motion integral never decreases. Readiness wait now asserts instead of silently timing out.
> - **Depth pass (user request after the stability work; `neutronCore/NeutronCoreShader.ts`, `NeutronCoreEngine.ts`):** driven by zoom depth (`uLinks`, 0 in CORE -> CORE unchanged): ~70 % of the non-structural envelope (non-member particles outside r~40-60) thins out at full depth; particles in front of the core's depth plane grow up to 2.6x and soften (near, out of focus), particles behind shrink/dim (far); the band around the core stays sharp; the existing swirl gives the parallax. Same particles, no new layer. `engine.setDepthFx(0|1)` is a verification-only A/B switch. New check `scripts/verify_brain_depth.mjs`.
> - **Verified in browser (Chrome/RTX 5050), final build:** lifecycle 18/18 steps (`verification/brain03/lifecycle_v2`): 0 labels/no interaction under Systems-over-BRAIN and at all 5 mid-reverse samples (CORE selected at d=400-814); mapping identical over 4 cycles with 1 assignment run; pivot glide 79.5 -> 0 over 122 intermediate frames, max step 4.9, monotonic; old record position not clickable; flow integral monotonic; 0 console errors. Continuity suite 60 s soak (`auto_v2`): GPU/CPU parity 4.7e-5/6.8e-5, pulse propagation 1.2 s vs 1.32 s designed (off: 0.02 s), clock never regressed over 18.5k frames, scene identity stable, reverse journey to plain CORE. Depth A/B (`verification/brain03/depth`, field annulus 150-360 px around the core): CORE identical (mean lum 2.3 vs 2.3); BRAIN lit-cell coverage 27.2 % -> 21.1 % (asserted >=15 % drop), peak/mean 12.4 -> 13.5; cell contrast 0.63 -> 0.58 (not improved; recorded, not asserted). `tsc` 0, `eslint` clean.
> - **Test-integrity notes:** the depth metric changed during development — a full-field peak/mean assertion failed (the intentional near/far softening lowers peaks), and a cell-contrast replacement also failed; instead of shopping for a passing metric, only coverage + CORE-unchanged are asserted and all other numbers are reported as measured. Record discoverability dropped from 2.07 to 1.72 after deterministic re-assignment (different carrier particles; still above the 1.35 threshold). The lifecycle suite was not run against the pre-fix code (no stash/reset allowed); the earlier `lifecycle_before` evidence shows the mid-journey leak that the new step-08 assertions reject.

> **BRAIN-03c/d/e: semantic structure, knowledge-label lifecycle fix, inward journey (2026-09-28, 09:10, Claude Code). Committed in `781c4b6`, pushed to `review/brain02-source-audit-20260927` (2026-09-28, 10:48). Builds on 03b below.**
> - **03c — "too uniform / no visible pulses / can't find nodes" (user review of 03b).** Filaments now have 3 tiers (`brain/atmosphereLinks.ts`): *ordinary* (sparse: nearest neighbour + rare 2nd/3rd), *local* (each real record keeps ≤4 brighter filaments to its neighbours), *relation* (a real graph link hub→record; the hub's linked records are carried by the hub's nearest neighbouring particles, so real relationships form a local cluster instead of long lines). Pulses: rhythmic bursts emitted from the hub/record end (bright head, fading tail; periods 4/8/16 s, exact 64 s wrap). Records: small tight-cored points with subtle hierarchy (hub 5.4 px, linked 4.8, other 4.4; heartbeat stronger for hubs). Identity: DOM label layer (`[data-brain-labels]`) shows only the hub label + labels near the cursor/hovered/connected; sidebar gained a "Nodes in field · hover to locate" list (hover → record highlight ring + label; click → note). Real data only: 35 records, 1 hub (GrowForge HQ, 12 real links); nothing fabricated.
> - **Bug found on the way (03c):** stale ordinary filaments accumulated into fog — hysteresis only killed an unwanted link once it had also stretched. Now an unwanted filament fades out after 2.5 s. Also carriers are assigned at BRAIN entry (records invisible in CORE).
> - **03d — label leak (user bug report: "GrowForge HQ Department" label visible in CORE and MISSIONS).** **Root cause:** the whole knowledge layer (labels, record points, pick targets) was gated only by camera distance (`brainRevealRef`), never by the active surface. Missions sits *deeper* than BRAIN (d≈72–89), so it satisfied the gate fully; the first part of every BRAIN→CORE journey is still inside BRAIN distance while CORE is already selected; the label layer (z-25) sits above the Missions overlay (z-20); hover ref/cursor/open note were never cleared on leaving. Reproduced in-browser before fixing (`verify_brain_lifecycle.mjs` → `verification/brain03/lifecycle_before`: labels + open note + pointer cursor in Missions, "GrowForge HQ Department" label with CORE selected). **Fix (`SpatialCanvas.tsx`, `BrainField.ts`, `SpatialHud.tsx`):** a smoothed *knowledge gate* driven by the surface (1 only when visualMode=brain, not diving, not travelling to another tier) multiplies labels + record points and enables picking; the label layer is `visibility:hidden` at 0; `clearBrainFocus()` (hover refs, list hover, field hover, cursor, open note) runs on nav away and on every gate 1→0 transition (covers wheel zoom-out and the Missions dive); HUD search closes/clears when the surface leaves BRAIN; the atmosphere reveal is 0 during the Missions dive/surface; travel eases `controls.target` back to the nucleus (a note's "focus node" could otherwise leave the orbit pivot off-centre) and the Missions dive ends centred. No per-node exclusions.
> - **03e — "journey must go inward, outer clutter out, core too bright, data revealed progressively" (user).** BRAIN camera now arrives at d=330 (was 460) so the inner field fills the frame. Link-capable particles are the near-core field (r 50–155, was 70–220). Ordinary filaments are never created beyond r=140 and fade 100→138; outer atmosphere (r>118) recedes as the field connects. Nucleus apparent size grows smoothly and monotonically ×1.3 (46→60 px radius) and calms (radial luminance, body alpha, inner corona reduced; extra −14 % body at BRAIN). Records fade in by importance (hub first, then linked, then the rest); hub label appears only near arrival (reveal ≥0.88). Palette unchanged (user: reference image = structure only, not colour).
> - **Verified (Chrome/RTX 5050; `scripts/verify_brain03_arbor.mjs` 60 s soak → `verification/brain03/auto`, `scripts/verify_brain_lifecycle.mjs` → `verification/brain03/lifecycle`):** lifecycle 12/12 — CORE→BRAIN (hover, open note)→MISSIONS→SYSTEMS→CORE→BRAIN, hover-only exit, exit via Missions: 0 labels / no pointer / no note / no search / not interactive outside BRAIN; knowledge field restored on re-entry; canvas/engine/field identity unchanged throughout. Arbor suite: journey connectivity monotonic, nucleus monotonic, no filament over cap (ordinary ≤52, key ≤34); ordinary filaments ~970 with midpoints r 48–141 (inner field, spread over 8 bins); records 2.0× more distinguishable than ordinary particles (local peak/mean contrast); pulses: +34–60 % luminance on a relation filament in the full scene, and with filaments isolated the brightness propagates along it with a 1.18 s lag vs 1.32 s designed (corr 0.71; with pulses off no propagation); labels sparse at rest (hub only), hover and sidebar-list locate work; click opens the real note; reverse journey dissolves to plain CORE; GPU↔CPU parity 4–9e-5; 0 console errors (unrelated `/api/jobs|approvals|agents` 500s during compile filtered). `tsc` 0, `eslint` clean.
> - **Test-infra lessons:** first propagation metric measured on the straight chord and missed the curved filament (flaky) — probes now use on-curve points from the same formula as the shader. The page now has 2 `<canvas>` elements, so scripts address the scene via `[data-brain-labels]`'s sibling container. The Systems panel does not close on Escape (test uses its × button). Dev server + tsc/eslint hit OOM at ~0.6 GB free RAM — run tsc/eslint with `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=3072`.
> - **Not verified / open:** lower quality tiers, reduced motion in BRAIN, mobile; hub label can overlap its own cluster filaments; `verify_brain01`/`verify_brain02a` hard-code d=460 and are stale; `verify_brain02b` obsolete. The "Good morning, Ony." CORE greeting seen in captures is not from this work.

> **BRAIN-03b: CORRECTION — one continuous atmosphere, no dendrite trunks (2026-09-28, 07:35, Claude Code). Committed in `781c4b6`, pushed to `review/brain02-source-audit-20260927` (2026-09-28, 10:48). SUPERSEDES the dendrite architecture in the BRAIN-03 entry below.**
> - **Why:** user reviewed the dendrite version and rejected it: it felt like "another outer layer was created" (a thin shell of promoted particles + a membrane) and "long cords suddenly emerge and cover the screen" (the 8 bright bundled trunks, ~12k ribbon vertices, growing across the whole frame). Required: the *already rotating* atmosphere particles connect over time with faint, thin, uneven, organic, pulsing filaments; connectivity grows with zoom; outer particles move faster as the dive deepens; nucleus grows smoothly; no abrupt giant structures.
> - **What was removed:** `arborSkeleton/arborRender/arborMath`, the trunk/ribbon/junction renderers, particle "recruitment" onto branches, the thin membrane band (r 116–146), the `BrainArbor` class. Real-record→branch-tip mapping is gone with them.
> - **What replaced it:** `brain/BrainField.ts` + `brain/atmosphereLinks.ts`. 900 link "members" are ordinary atmosphere particles (same radius law r=70+u^1.3·150 over the whole atmosphere, no band; the remaining atmosphere is drawn from the same law, so CORE looks unchanged). Filaments are **GPU-instanced**, 8-segment sinuous curves between members chosen by CPU-replica kNN (2 nearest always, 3rd/4th/5th rarely → irregular, not a lattice), max length 44 units, slowly breathing, each with a gentle travelling data pulse (speed k/64 → exact 64 s wrap, no restart). Each filament has its own reveal threshold that **rises with its radius**, so connectivity spreads outward from the dense inner atmosphere as you zoom (no early detached fringe), and reach grows with zoom. Real records (35) ride 35 distinct real atmosphere particles (stable hash of the canonical id, radius 80–190), drawn as small (3.2 px) points that appear mid-zoom, with invisible 11-unit pick spheres; hub links are only shown through hover highlight (no fabricated geometry).
> - **Journey:** 5.8 s (was 4.2, originally 2.4), decelerating dolly; connectivity = f(camera distance 800→470); swirl speed-up peaks mid-dive (×3.6 flow) then settles to ×1.4 so records stay clickable (first attempt kept full speed at BRAIN and the click missed — found by the browser test); nucleus apparent size grows monotonically (+~38 % 880→460); coherent-rotation blend RIGID_OMEGA 0.07 (natural atmosphere mean) up to 0.55 so filaments don't shear apart.
> - **Verified (Chrome/RTX 5050, `scripts/verify_brain03_arbor.mjs`, 60 s soak, frames in `growforge-ui/verification/brain03/auto` and `v2`):** forward journey — connectivity 0→1 monotonic, first appears at d≈750, longest filament ≤44 at every sample (no cords); reverse journey — dissolves monotonically back to plain CORE (links 0, d 880); re-entry fully connected; GPU↔CPU particle parity 7e-5 units in CORE and BRAIN; swirl 0.99 → 3.34 peak → 1.38 settled; hover pointer + click opens the real note; layer filter survives refreshes; identity of canvas/engine/field stable; clock monotonic; 0 console/page errors (only unrelated 500s from `/api/jobs|approvals|agents` while Next compiled, filtered). `tsc` 0, `eslint` clean, `git diff --check` clean. BRAIN-01 script passed 7/7 earlier in this session on the previous build (not re-run after 3b).
> - **Measured cost:** relink 3.1 ms CPU (median) every 0.7 s; steady BRAIN frame cadence identical to plain CORE (median 6.9 ms, 3 frames/12 s >20 ms in both) — those hitches are baseline, not from BRAIN. The snap monitor's max "speed" (~230 u/s) is a measurement artefact of those baseline hitches (engine advances a full 30–40 ms in one frame), not a discontinuity.
> - **Not verified / gaps:** medium/low quality tiers, reduced motion in BRAIN, narrow/mobile layouts, weaker GPUs; the speed/settle feel and how faint the filaments read on other monitors are visual judgements for the user; `verify_brain02a` still hard-codes the old 2.4 s (fails Tests 1/3 unmodified, 6/6 with widened windows on the previous build), `verify_brain02b` is obsolete (tests the deleted OrganicNeuralField).

> **BRAIN-03: PARTICLE → MEMBRANE → DENDRITE ARBOR — rewrite of the BRAIN scene (2026-09-28, 07:05, Claude Code). Committed in `781c4b6`, pushed to `review/brain02-source-audit-20260927` (2026-09-28, 10:48).**
> - **Why:** Antigravity's BRAIN-02B (`OrganicNeuralField`: ~35 anchors linked node-to-node by curves) read as a graph *around* the core, not the core's own particles organising (user's cb.mp4 reference + Gemini/ChatGPT audit). User approved a full architectural replacement; Claude Code was sole writer. My own first attempt (tuning the old graph: smaller anchors, fades) was rejected by the user as not the target, then superseded.
> - **Architecture (new):** one shared, deterministic particle source. `neutronCore/coreParticleField.ts` generates the CORE buffers (identity = buffer index; 820 membrane "members" occupy indices 0..819 at every quality tier) + a TS replica of the shader motion; `neutronCore/coreMotionGlsl.ts` is the single GLSL `coreParticleWorld()` used by the CORE points, membrane links, dendrite ribbons and junctions; the engine exposes its uniforms (`engine.shared`) by reference so all shaders are on the same state. `brain/arborSkeleton.ts` = deterministic 8-trunk tree (trunks → 2 bundled companions → side branches → hairs, ~6k skeleton points / 12k ribbon vertices); `brain/membraneLinks.ts` = kNN links between member particles (CPU replica picks pairs 1.4×/s with hysteresis, GPU positions them); `brain/arborRender.ts` = camera-facing tapered fibers, restrained trunk bodies, junction points; `brain/BrainArbor.ts` = orchestration, gate binding, real-record mapping, picking. Growth runs inward *and* outward from a "gate" where each trunk crosses the CORE shell, and each trunk is bound to a real member particle there. ~55% of non-member atmosphere particles are *recruited* (glide onto branches in the CORE shader). Real records: 35 nodes → junctions; hub agent (12 real links) sits on a fork with its linked records on that fork's sub-tree; only real links are encoded, decorative junctions carry no data.
> - **Bug found & fixed on the way (affects CORE too):** the old CORE shader rotated each particle by `angSpeed(perturbedRadius) × ever-growing time`, so the swirl wobble grew the longer the page stayed open (~0.24 rad after 10 min at 130 units). Angular speed is now from the unperturbed radius (exactly linear in time). Also: outer shell blends into a slow shared rigid neural frame in BRAIN via monotonic accumulators (`flowTotal`/`flowBlend`) — no snaps when the blend changes.
> - **Also fixed:** routine 12 s data refresh no longer resets the user's layer filter (`knownCategoryIdsRef`); CORE→BRAIN journey is 4.2 s with a decelerating dolly (was 2.4 s); emergence is driven by camera distance 800→470 (works for wheel zoom and reverses); BRAIN sidebar now appears at zoom progress ≥ 0.93 (was 0.85) so it follows the structure; nucleus grows ×1.2 at BRAIN. Removed the dead growth-replay effect (no UI triggered it) and `OrganicNeuralField.ts`.
> - **Verified in a real browser (RTX 5050, Chrome, `scripts/verify_brain03_arbor.mjs`, 60 s soak, captures in `growforge-ui/verification/brain03/`):** GPU↔CPU parity via WebGL2 transform feedback in CORE and BRAIN state (max error 1.2e-4 / 1.1e-4 units); 35/35 records each on a unique junction; membrane visible before growth begins; ~90 s total in BRAIN incl. CORE round trip and re-entry; 0 console/page errors; canvas/engine/arbor identity unchanged across ≥6 data refreshes; animation clock never regresses; max per-frame movement 0.6 units (no snap); membrane edges bounded (~1.77k); hover → pointer, click opens the real note ("GrowForge HQ Department — 12 links"); MCP layer toggle survives refreshes. Existing gates: `tsc` 0 errors; `eslint` clean; `git diff --check` clean; BRAIN-01 script 7/7.
> - **Existing scripts that now need updating (not regressions):** `verify_brain02a_journey_repairs.mjs` hard-codes the 2.4 s journey (fixed sampling windows) — with windows widened it passes 6/6, unmodified it fails Tests 1/3; its Test 2 recomputes the nucleus formula itself rather than reading the engine. `verify_brain02b_organic_neural_field.mjs` tests the deleted architecture and is obsolete.
> - **NOT verified / known gaps:** medium/low quality-tier rebuild and reduced-motion in BRAIN (implemented, not exercised); mobile/narrow layouts; low-end GPU performance (median frame 6.9 ms on the RTX 5050 only; per-frame CPU cost of relink/binding not profiled); recruit-glide and ribbon gate-binding visuals have no numeric test (eyeballed); one Playwright frame (during the first journey) showed the sidebar "GRAPH LAYERS" chips missing — not reproduced in any soak sample (chips=6 every time), cause unknown. Subjective: nucleus is white/over-exposed at BRAIN and trunks read as bright smooth bands; may want a taste pass.

> **BRAIN-02A: CINEMATIC JOURNEY REPAIR & RENDERING FIDELITY (2026-09-27, 16:30, Antigravity).**
> - **Objective:** Repaired the mounted CORE ↔ BRAIN continuous journey controllers, rendering fidelity, and motion staging before implementing the organic neural-particle network (BRAIN-02B).
> - **Delivered Architectural & Visual Repairs:**
>   1. **Mutual Exclusion & Clean Interruption (`SpatialCanvas.tsx`):**
>      - Unified transition cancellation with `cancelTransitions(restoreCamera)`.
>      - Missions dive (`DIVE_MS = 1150`) and Brain travel (`BRAIN_JOURNEY_MS = 2400`) are strictly mutually exclusive.
>      - Wheel scroll, pointer drag, and touch gestures cancel active transitions instantly and smoothly yield control to `OrbitControls` with zero camera jumps or position snaps (`jumpDelta = 0`).
>   2. **Coordinated 3-Stage 2.4s Journey Staging (`SpatialCanvas.tsx`, `SpatialHud.tsx`):**
>      - **Stage 1: Acceleration (0.0 - 0.35 tau, d: 880 -> 620):** Dock / Command Center fades out during the first 20% of the dive; sidebar remains strictly hidden; graph `depthProgress = 0.0` (100% hidden).
>      - **Stage 2: Dive, Parallax & Progressive Reveal (0.35 - 0.75 tau, d: 620 -> 500):** Peak foreground particle velocity; subtle +3.2° FOV lens breathing; hot-white node cores emerge at $d \le 620$ followed by outer somas and cyan interconnecting links at $d \le 540$.
>      - **Stage 3: Deceleration & Arrival (0.75 - 1.0 tau, d: 500 -> 460):** Camera settles gently into Brain orbit at $d = 460$; FOV locks at 48°; Brain Knowledge Architecture sidebar slides in gracefully from the left at $\tau \ge 0.85$ ($coreZoomProgress \ge 0.85$).
>   3. **Nucleus Depth Scale & Overshoot Prevention (`NeutronCoreEngine.ts`):**
>      - Fixed depth scaling: for $d \ge 460$, $depthScale = 0.52 + (d - 460) \times (0.40 / 420)$; for $d < 460$ (Missions approach), $depthScale = (d / 460) \times 0.52$.
>      - Angular size ratio: Brain/Core = 1.08x, Missions/Core = 1.08x (100% constant, strictly eliminating close-up screen-filling ballooning or overshoot).
